## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 12:26:38 PM</sup>

I've reviewed the repo context (`doc/fix`, `src/fix/vhdl`, `src/fix/python/olo_fix`, `test/fix`). Nothing named `lin_approx` exists yet, so this is a green-field entity.

Before I start, I need the spec. Your branch is `feature/lin-approx`, so I'm guessing a piecewise-linear function approximation entity, but I don't want to design from a guess. Please answer:

**1. Entity name**
My assumption: `olo_fix_lin_approx`. Confirm or correct.

**2. Functionality**
What exactly should it compute? My assumption: piecewise-linear approximation of an arbitrary function `y = f(x)`, using the MSBs of the input as a table index (offset + slope per segment) and the remaining LSBs as the interpolation fraction, i.e. `y = Offset[idx] + Slope[idx] * frac`.

Open points I need pinned down:
- Where do the coefficients come from? A generic (real vector / file), a runtime-writable port, or generated from a Python-provided function?
- Number of segments: always a power of two (simple MSB indexing), or arbitrary?
- Input range handling: is the input assumed in-range, or should out-of-range inputs be clamped?
- Is there a fixed set of built-in functions (sin, log2, 1/x, sqrt, ...) or is it purely coefficient-driven?

**3. Architectures**
Only one implementation, or several (e.g. a fully parallel LUT-in-logic variant vs. a RAM-based variant, and/or a TDM/multi-channel variant)? If several, how are they selected (generic string like `Resource_g` / separate entities)?

**4. Generics**
My assumption, for you to amend:
`InFmt_g`, `OutFmt_g`, `OffsetFmt_g`, `SlopeFmt_g`, plus segment count / coefficient source, `Round_g`, `Saturate_g`, `RoundReg_g`, `SatReg_g`, and RAM controls (`RamStyle_g`, `RamBehavior_g`).
Anything to add or drop?

**5. Ports**
My assumption: `Clk`, `Rst`, `In_Valid`, `In_Data`, `Out_Valid`, `Out_Result` (the standard olo_fix pattern). Do you need anything extra, e.g. a coefficient-write interface (`Cfg_*`), `In_Last`/`Out_Last` for TDM, or a multi-channel index port?

Once you confirm these I'll build the full set: VHDL, bit-true Python model + 100% covered unit tests, testbench + cosim, `sim/test_configs/olo_fix.py`, `tools/inference_test/yaml/fix.yml`, docs and the `EntityList.md` link, then run VUnit, VSG and coverage.

# Rivierapro workaround: VUnit's format_generic only quotes values containing spaces,
# but Rivierapro parses unquoted values like "(1,8,4)" as VHDL aggregates instead of
# strings, rejecting them and falling back to the default generic value.
# Quoting values that contain '(', ')', or ',' fixes this.
if os.environ.get('VUNIT_SIMULATOR') == 'rivierapro':
    import vunit.sim_if.rivierapro as _rp
    def _rivierapro_format_generic(value):
        value_str = str(value)
        if any(c in value_str for c in '(), '):

import os
import importlib

# Get the current directory
current_dir = os.path.dirname(__file__)

# Recursively import all Python files in subdirectories
for root, dirs, files in os.walk(current_dir):
    for file in files:
        if file.endswith(".py") and file != "__init__.py":
            # Construct the module name relative to the package
            relative_path = os.path.relpath(os.path.join(root, file), current_dir)
            module_name = relative_path.replace(os.sep, ".")[:-3]  # Remove ".py" extension

            try:
                # Import the module dynamically
                importlib.import_module(f".{module_name}", package=__name__)
            except ImportError as e:

```
def cl_fix_resize(a, a_fmt : FixFormat,
                  r_fmt : FixFormat,
                  rnd : FixRound = FixRound.Trunc_s, sat : FixSaturate = FixSaturate.None_s):
    """
    Resizes data values (with rounding, then saturation) to fit a new fixed-point format.
    """
    # Round
    rounded_fmt = FixFormat.for_round(a_fmt, r_fmt.F, rnd)
    rounded = cl_fix_round(a, a_fmt, rounded_fmt, rnd)
    
    # Saturate
    result = cl_fix_saturate(rounded, rounded_fmt, r_fmt, sat)

    return result

###################################################################################################
# Arithmetic operations
###################################################################################################

def cl_fix_abs(a, a_fmt : FixFormat,
               r_fmt : FixFormat = None,
               rnd : FixRound = FixRound.Trunc_s, sat : FixSaturate = FixSaturate.None_s):
    """

# olo_fix_pkg_writer

[Back to **Entity List**](../EntityList.md)

## Status Information

This is a pure Python utility and therefore does not come with VHDL simulation status.

Python Source: [olo_fix_pkg_writer](../../src/fix/python/olo_fix_pkg_writer.py)

## Usage

A detailed example for the usage of _olo_fix_pkg_writer_ can be found in the
[OloFixTutorial](../tutorials/OloFixTutorial.md). Below is a brief example for the usage of _olo_fix_pkg_writer_.

The package is used as shown below:

```python
someFormat = FixFormat(1, 8, 23)
...
writer = olo_fix_pkg_writer()
writer.add_constant("SomeInt", int, 5)
writer.add_constant("SomeFormat", FixFormat, someFormat) # Constant of FixFormat are not allowed for Verilog
writer.add_constant("SomeFormatStr", FixFormat, someFormat, as_string=True)
writer.add_vector("SomeIntVector", int, [1, 2, 3])
# Write VHDL package
writer.write_vhdl_pkg("my_pkg", out_dir)
# Write Verilog header
writer.write_veri
...(truncated)
```

Any function can be\napproximated by a table containing the function value (_offset_) and its derivative (_gradient_) for regularly spaced\npoints, plus linear interpolation between those points.\n\nThis is not just one component but a whole family of components. All approximations share the same HDL implementation\nof the calculation ([olo_fix_lin_approx_calc](./olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.md)) and only differ in number formats and\ntable content. The HDL is therefore not written by hand but generated from Python.\n\nThe typical workflow is:\n\n1. Describe the approximation in a configuration object (_olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg_).\n2. Iterate on the settings using _analyze()_ until the accuracy and the resource usage fit the needs.\n3. Generate the VHDL entity (_generate_entity()_) and - optionally - a bit-true testbench (_generate_tb()_).\n4. ```
doc/fix/olo_fix_lin_approx.md stripped 0
doc/fix/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.md stripped 1
the value of the function at the **center** of the segment (_offset_) and the derivative of the function at the same
point (_gradient_).

```text
 f(x)
  ^                                          .-'
  |                                       .-'
  |            offset[i] o------------.-'|
  |                            _.,--'    |  gradient[i] = df/dx at the segment center
  |                      _.,--'          |
  |                _.,--'                |
  |          _.,--'                      |
  +------|---------|---------|-----------|---------> x
         |<- segment i ->|
         |         ^
   segment start   segment center
```

The output is calculated as:

```text
Out_Result = offset[index] + gradient[index] * (In_Data - segment_center[index])
```

The upper _log2(TableSize_g)_ bits of _In_Data_ are used as table index, the remaining lower bits are the position
```

</details>

## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 1:01:14 PM</sup>

Done. All three gates pass: simulation, VSG lint, and 100% Python coverage.

## What was built

**Production VHDL** — [olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd](src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd)
Port of `psi_fix_lin_approx_calc`: same 7-stage pipeline, same index/remainder split with MSB inversion, full-precision multiply and add (DSP-friendly), round/saturate in a separate output stage. Table stays external via `Tbl_Addr`/`Tbl_Data`.

**Python model + generator** — [olo_fix_lin_approx.py](src/fix/python/olo_fix/olo_fix_lin_approx.py)
`olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg` (config container) and `olo_fix_lin_approx` (bit-true model, `analyze()`, `generate_entity()`, `generate_tb()`). Code generation is jinja2 via [two templates](src/fix/python/olo_fix/templates/); the custom `<TOKEN>` replacement is gone.

**Verification** — [lin_approx_codegen.py](test/fix/olo_fix_lin_approx/lin_approx_codegen.py) defines four sample approximations covering every signedness combination: `sqrt_u2u`, `sin_u2s`, `tanh_s2s`, `square_s2u` (the last also exercises `Trunc_s` rounding and output saturation). Following the `olo_fix_pkg_writer` pattern, [sim/codegen.py](sim/codegen.py) generates entities, testbenches and `.fix` files before VUnit collects files. The generated TBs use `olo_test_fix_stimuli_vc`/`olo_test_fix_checker_vc` with FullSpeed and Throttled runs, like every other olo_fix testbench. 8/8 pass; full fix suite 1142/1142.

**Also**: [test_olo_fix_lin_approx.py](src/fix/python/tests/test_olo_fix_lin_approx.py) (27 tests, 100% statements), inference-test entry, both doc pages, EntityList links, compile_order.

## Deviations from psi_fix worth your review

- **scipy removed** as asked. `_derivative()` is a central difference with `dx=1e-6`, matching `scipy.misc.derivative`'s default `order=3`.
- **Built-in CONFIGS not ported.** psi_fix shipped `Sin18Bit`/`Sqrt18Bit`/`Gaussify20Bit`/`Invert18Bit` inside the model; `Gaussify` needed `scipy.stats.norm.ppf`. I kept the production class config-driven and put the example configurations in the test area. Say the word if you want a set of standard configurations shipped in the library.
- **`Round_g`/`Saturate_g` added** as generics (defaults `NonSymPos_s`/`Sat_s` = psi_fix's hardcoded behaviour).
- **`IntFmt`/`AddFmt` use `cl_fix_mult_fmt`/`cl_fix_add_fmt`** rather than psi_fix's hand-derived formulas. Both are lossless so results are identical; psi_fix carried one redundant integer bit.
- **Default `valid_range`** is the full input-format range, not psi_fix's `(0, inf)`.
- The generated ROM initializer needs a `-- vsg_off variable_400` guard: VSG wants continuation lines indented to 74 columns, which is not sensible for a 256+ entry table.
- I did **not** add the new file to `src/fix/olo_fix_dev.core` — git history shows the `.core` files are only touched in "DOC: update files for release" commits. Note that `olo_fix_fir_dec_semi_chtdm.vhd` from the previous commit on this branch is also absent there.

## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 1:06:33 PM</sup>

Yes, you're right. `RemFmt_c.F` is `InFmt_c.F` by construction (line 81), so `InFmt_c.F - RemFmt_c.F` is always 0 and the whole expression collapses to `-RemFmt_c.I`. It's carried over verbatim from psi_fix, where the same redundancy exists.

{
  "command": "python3 - <<'EOF'\np=\"src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd\"\ns=open(p).read()\nold = \"\"\"    -- Table index (unsigned, negative inputs wrap into the upper half of the table)\n    constant IdxFmt_c       : FixFormat_t := (0, InFmt_c.S + InFmt_c.I,\n                                              InFmt_c.F - RemFmt_c.F - RemFmt_c.I);\"\"\"\nnew = \"\"\"    -- Table index (unsigned, negative inputs wrap into the upper half of the table)\n    -- The index is the input shifted right by the number of remainder bits, hence F = -RemFmt_c.I\n    constant IdxFmt_c       : FixFormat_t := (0, InFmt_c.S + InFmt_c.I, -RemFmt_c.I);\"\"\"\nassert old in s\nopen(p,\"w\").write(s.replace(old,new,1))\n\np=\"src/fix/python/olo_fix/olo_fix_lin_approx.py\"\ns=open(p).read()\nold = \"\"\"        # Table index (unsigned, negative inputs wrap into the upper half of the table)\n        self._idx_fmt = FixFormat(0, cfg.in_fmt.S + cfg.in_fmt.I,\n                                  cfg.in_fmt.F - self._rem_fmt.F - self._rem_fmt.I)\"\"\"\nnew = \"\"\"        # Table index (unsigned, negative inputs wrap into the upper half of the table)\n        # The index is the input shifted right by the number of remainder bits, hence F = -rem_fmt.I\n        self._idx_fmt = FixFormat(0, cfg.in_fmt.S + cfg.in_fmt.I, -self._rem_fmt.I)\"\"\"\nassert old in s\nopen(p,\"w\").write(s.replace(old,new,1))\nprint(\"ok\")\nEOF\nsed -n '84,88p' src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd"

## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 1:16:05 PM</sup>

Done. The arithmetic now goes through the olo_fix entities.

## Changes in [olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd](src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd)

The two-process block now only covers the input splitting and the table interface (`Valid(0 to 3)`). Everything after the table read is instantiated:

| Instance | Entity | Settings |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `i_offs_delay` | `olo_fix_private_optional_reg` | `Stages_g => MultRegs_g` — aligns the offset with the multiplier output |
| `i_mult` | `olo_fix_mult` | `ResultFmt_g => IntFmt_c`, `OpRegs_g => MultRegs_g`, `RoundReg_g/SatReg_g => "NO"` |
| `i_add` | `olo_fix_add` | `ResultFmt_g => AddFmt_c`, `OpRegs_g => 1`, `RoundReg_g/SatReg_g => "NO"` |
| `i_resize` | `olo_fix_resize` | `Round_g`/`Saturate_g`, `RoundReg_g/SatReg_g => "YES"` |

`IntFmt_c` and `AddFmt_c` are exactly the internal formats of `olo_fix_mult`/`olo_fix_add` (`cl_fix_mult_fmt` / `cl_fix_add_fmt`), so both internal resizes are pass-throughs and I set their round/sat registers to `"NO"` — otherwise they'd add latency for a no-op. The output `olo_fix_resize` keeps both registers enabled as you said.

New generic: `MultRegs_g : natural := 1`, matching the `olo_fix_madd` / `olo_fix_fir_dec_semi_chtdm` naming.

**Latency is now `MultRegs_g + 7`** (8 with defaults, was 7) — the output round and saturate are two registers instead of one.

## Propagated

- Both templates gained `MultRegs_g`: the generated wrapper forwards it to `olo_fix_lin_approx_calc`, and the generated TB forwards it to the DUT.
- [sim/test_configs/olo_fix.py](sim/test_configs/olo_fix.py) now runs each sample with `MultRegs_g` in {0, 1, 3} — 24 lin_approx tests, all bit-true against the same reference files, which confirms latency independence.
- Docs updated: latency formula, `MultRegs_g` row, and the pipeline stage table now shows the variable-length multiply stage and the split round/saturate stages.
- `compile_order.txt` regenerated (the entity moved after its new dependencies).

Verification: 1158/1158 fix sims pass, VSG clean on the entity and all generated files, 209 Python tests with 100% coverage on the new module.

## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 1:19:07 PM</sup>

Because the two components have different delay semantics, and only one of them matches the multiplier pipeline.

**`olo_base_delay` delays by N *valid samples*; `olo_fix_private_optional_reg` delays by N *clock cycles*.**

Every stage in `olo_base_delay` is gated — [olo_base_delay.vhd:93](src/base/vhdl/olo_base_delay.vhd#L93) in the SRL path and [olo_base_delay.vhd:175](src/base/vhdl/olo_base_delay.vhd#L175) in the output register both sit inside `if In_Valid = '1' then`. The pipeline inside `olo_fix_mult` is `olo_fix_private_optional_reg`, which shifts unconditionally on every clock and carries the valid flag alongside the data.

So the offset would only stay aligned while `In_Valid` is continuous. With a gap it breaks — `MultRegs_g = 2`, valid at cycles 0 and 2:

| Cycle | `olo_fix_mult` (free-running) | `olo_base_delay` (valid-gated) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 2 | `Mult_Valid='1'`, `Mult_Data = s0` | `Out_Data` still 0 — its update fires *at* cycle 2, visible at 3 |
| 4 | `Mult_Valid='1'`, `Mult_Data = s1` | `Out_Data = s0` |

The adder would pair `s0`'s gradient product with the wrong offset. That is exactly the case the `Throttled` test exercises, so it would be a latent bug that only shows up under stalls.

Two secondary reasons:

- **Resource intent.** For `Delay_g > 1` with `Resource_g="AUTO"`, `olo_base_delay` actively forces shift-register inference (`srl_style => "srl"`, `ramstyle => "MLAB"`, [olo_base_delay.vhd:84-86](src/base/vhdl/olo_base_delay.vhd#L84-L86)). These 0-3 registers feed a DSP adder input — you want plain flip-flops that can be packed into the slice, not an SRL.
- **Consistency.** `olo_fix_private_optional_reg` is what `olo_fix_mult`, `olo_fix_add`, `olo_fix_round`, `olo_fix_saturate`, `olo_fix_abs`, `olo_fix_neg`, `olo_fix_sub`, `olo_fix_addsub` and `olo_fix_compare` all use for their register generics, so latency matching with `OpRegs_g => MultRegs_g` is structural rather than something I have to reason about.

I could have tied `In_Valid => '1'` on an `olo_base_delay` to turn it into a plain cycle delay, but that still leaves the SRL forcing and the unused `RstState_g` counter logic, for no gain.

Unrelated but worth noting while we're in that file: the header comment of [olo_fix_private_optional_reg.vhd:11-13](src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_private_optional_reg.vhd#L11-L13) says it "contains synthesis attributes that prevent retiming" — grep shows the file has no attribute declarations at all, so either the attributes were dropped at some point or the comment is stale. Pre-existing, not something I touched.

## Assistant <sup>9/2/2026, 1:21:53 PM</sup>

Two things — one correction, then yes to your substantive point.

**The correction:** `i_offs_delay` doesn't delay the valid flag. It delays `r.Offs_3`, the offset read from the table ([line 192](src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd#L192)) — `width(OffsFmt_g)` bits of data that go straight into `In_A` of `i_add`. Its `Out_Valid` is `open` precisely because the valid comes from the multiplier instead. So it is a pure data delay, but the data is a DSP operand, not bookkeeping.

**Your point:** yes, you're right that `olo_base_delay` works here, and my alignment objection was aimed at the wrong thing. With `In_Valid => '1'` it becomes a plain N-cycle delay and the valid-gating hazard I described disappears entirely. `Delay_g = 0` is handled too ([g_zero](src/base/vhdl/olo_base_delay.vhd#L163-L165)), so `MultRegs_g = 0` still works.

Two settings would be needed for it to behave as intended:

- `In_Valid => '1'` — otherwise it delays by N *valid samples* and misaligns under stalls
- `RstState_g => False` — otherwise the `RstStateCnt` counter runs on every clock and forces zeros out for the first `Delay_g - 1` cycles after reset. Harmless (valid is low then), but it is logic for nothing.

And on SRLs: I agree they'd be acceptable functionally. In practice nothing would be inferred anyway — at `Delay_g = 2` the SRL path builds `MemTaps_c = 1` stage, at `Delay_g = 3` it builds 2, and no tool maps a depth-1 or depth-2 shift register into an SRL. So the `srl_style` attribute is inert at these depths.

**My recommendation is still to keep `olo_fix_private_optional_reg`,** for two reasons that are about robustness rather than resources:

1. It is correct with no generic tuning. The `olo_base_delay` version is only correct if both `In_Valid => '1'` and `RstState_g => False` are set, and if someone later "fixes" `In_Valid` to `r.Valid(3)` because it looks like an oversight, the design breaks silently under stalls.
2. `OpRegs_g => MultRegs_g` on the multiplier and `Stages_g => MultRegs_g` on the delay are then the same component, so the latency match is structural.

That said, this is your library and your call on the convention — if you'd rather reserve `olo_fix_private_optional_reg` strictly for arithmetic pipelines and use `olo_base_delay` for plain delays, say so and I'll switch it (with `In_Valid => '1'`, `RstState_g => False`) and re-run the regression.

`In_Valid => '1'` and `RstState_g => false` as discussed, with a comment on why the valid is tied high so it doesn't get "corrected" later. Defaults are kept for `Resource_g`/`RamStyle_g`/`RamBehavior_g` since the delay is 0-3 stages.

Verified: 24/24 lin_approx tests pass across `MultRegs_g` in {0, 1, 3} — covering the `g_zero` (Delay_g=0), `g_single` (Delay_g=1) and `g_srl` (Delay_g=3) paths of `olo_base_delay` — and 1158/1158 for the whole fix suite. VSG clean, compile order unchanged.

## Assistant <sup>9/4/2026, 11:17:02 AM</sup>

Restructured. [olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd](src/fix/vhdl/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.vhd) is back to a single two-process datapath with `olo_fix_resize` as the only instance.

**Record shape** — the pipeline depths track `MultRegs_g`:

```vhdl
    type TwoProcess_r is record
        Valid    : std_logic_vector(0 to MultRegs_g + 4);
        In_0     : std_logic_vector(In_Data'range);
        TblIdx_1 : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(IdxFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
        Remain   : Rem_t(1 to 3);
        Grad_3   : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(GradFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
        Offs     : Offs_t(3 to MultRegs_g + 3);
        MulVal   : Mul_t(4 to MultRegs_g + 3);
        Add      : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(AddFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
    end record;
```

`Offs` carries one more element than `MulVal`, so `r.Offs'high` and `r.MulVal'high` are the same stage and the adder reads both by `'high` — the offset delay is just the array depth, no separate component.

**Multiplier inputs are both registered**: `r.Grad_3` (stage-3 table register) and `r.Remain(3)` (end of the remainder pipe), so the DSP input registers can be used. Noted in a comment so it survives future edits.

**`MultRegs_g` is now `positive := 1`.** With everything in the two-process record, every record member is a register, so `MultRegs_g = 0` would need the product routed around the array as a variable — clunky, and it would also leave the multiplier output unregistered, defeating DSP packing. `olo_fix_fir_dec_semi_chtdm` already uses `positive := 1`; `olo_fix_madd` says `natural` but indexes `MulReg(MultRegs_g - 1)`, so it does not support 0 either. Test configs moved from {0, 1, 3} to **{1, 2, 3}**.

Latency is unchanged at `MultRegs_g + 7` (5 two-process registers + 2 in `olo_fix_resize`).

Propagated to both templates (`positive := 1`), the test configs, and both doc pages — the architecture table no longer points at `olo_fix_mult`/`olo_fix_add`, and gained a note on the registered multiplier inputs.

Verified: VSG clean on the entity and all 8 generated files, 24/24 lin_approx sims, 1158/1158 for the full fix suite, 202 Python tests at 100% coverage.

`MultRegs_g` is gone. The pipeline is now fixed-depth:

```vhdl
    type TwoProcess_r is record
        Valid    : std_logic_vector(0 to 5);
        In_0     : std_logic_vector(In_Data'range);
        TblIdx_1 : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(IdxFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
        Remain   : Rem_t(1 to 3);
        Grad_3   : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(GradFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
        Offs     : Offs_t(3 to 4);
        MulVal_4 : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(MulFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
        Add_5    : std_logic_vector(cl_fix_width(AddFmt_c) - 1 downto 0);
    end record;
```

`Mul_t` is dropped (the product is a single register now), `Offs` is a plain 2-element delay, and both multiplier inputs stay registered. Latency is a fixed **8 clock cycles** (6 two-process registers + 2 in `olo_fix_resize`).

## User <sup>9/4/2026, 11:26:51 AM</sup>

```
fixed
### Precision

The multiplication and the addition are executed at full precision (no rounding, no saturation). This allows the adder
to be implemented within a DSP slice. Rounding and saturation are applied in separate pipeline stages at the output,
controlled by _Round_g_ and _Saturate_g_.

The approximation error depends on the number of table entries and the formats chosen for the table. The Python class
[olo_fix_lin_approx](./olo_fix_lin_approx.md) provides an _analyze()_ method that helps finding suitable settings.
```

## Assistant <sup>9/4/2026, 11:29:31 AM</sup>

Yes — and the separate function was worse than redundant. `_used_indices()` is deleted, replaced by one line in `analyze()`:

```python
        # Table ranges of the entries actually hit by the stimuli (helps choosing offs_fmt/grad_fmt)
        used = np.unique(self._table_index(in_data))
```

`in_data` is already quantized by `stimuli()`, so `_table_index()` takes it directly.

Three things this fixes beyond being 10 lines shorter:

1. **It respected `sim_range`.** `_used_indices()` always read `cfg.valid_range`, so `analyze(sim_range=...)` printed table ranges for a different range than it plotted. With `sqrt_u2u` and `sim_range=(0.5, 0.75)`: 65 indices actually hit vs. 192 reported, gradient range `0.576 .. 0.706` vs. `0.501 .. 0.996`. That's exactly the number you'd use to size `grad_fmt`, so it was misleading in the one place it mattered.

2. **It crashed.** `cl_fix_from_real(scalar, fmt)` returns a 0-d array, so `self._table_index(...)[0]` raised `IndexError: too many indices for array: array is 0-dimensional`. The function could never have run. It was excluded from coverage and never exercised by the regression, so nothing caught it — my fault for writing it that way.

3. **The wrap special-case was unnecessary.** Collecting the indices actually hit handles the signed upper-half wrap for free.

Verified equivalent where the old version was meant to apply — for the full valid range, both give identical index sets (256/256 for the wrapping signed case, 192/192 for `sqrt_u2u`) and identical gradient ranges. `analyze()` now runs end to end on both a full and a restricted range.

202 Python tests pass, still 100% coverage (now 71 statements). No test changes were needed since `analyze()` is excluded.

## User <sup>9/4/2026, 11:42:09 AM</sup>

<details>
<summary>Tool Result</summary>

```
ok
```

The table index and the output are calculated as follows (_S_ and _I_ being the sign and integer bits of _InFmt_g_):

```text
segment_width  = 2**(S + I) / TableSize_g
index          = floor(In_Data / segment_width) mod TableSize_g
segment_center = (floor(In_Data / segment_width) + 0.5) * segment_width

Out_Result     = offset[index] + gradient[index] * (In_Data - segment_center)
```

The upper _log2(TableSize_g)_ bits of _In_Data_ are used as table index, the remaining lower bits are the position
within the segment. The lower bits are unsigned and relative to the beginning of the segment. By inverting their MSB,
they are converted into the signed offset relative to the center of the segment - which is exactly what the
multiplication above requires.
```

</details>

  "command": "cd src/fix/python && python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport sys, os, numpy as np\nsys.path.append(os.path.abspath(\".\"))\nfrom olo_fix import olo_fix_lin_approx, olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg\nfrom en_cl_fix_pkg import *\n\ndef check(name, cfg):\n    a = olo_fix_lin_approx(cfg)\n    f = cfg.in_fmt\n    seg_w = 2.0**(f.S + f.I) / cfg.points\n    x = a.stimuli(5000)                      # quantized to in_fmt\n\n    # Doc formulas\n    doc_index  = np.floor(x / seg_w).astype(int) % cfg.points\n    doc_center = (np.floor(x / seg_w) + 0.5) * seg_w\n    doc_out    = a.offs_table[doc_index] + a.grad_table[doc_index] * (x - doc_center)\n\n    # Model\n    mdl_index  = a._table_index(x)\n    mdl_center = a.centers[mdl_index]\n    mdl_out    = a.process(x)\n\n    print(f\"{name:12s} seg_width={seg_w:<12g} index match={np.array_equal(doc_index, mdl_index)} \"\n          f\"center match={np.allclose(doc_center, mdl_center)} \"\n          f\"out max-diff={np.max(np.abs(doc_out - mdl_out))*2**cfg.out_fmt.F:.2f} LSB (quantization only)\")\n\ncheck(\"sqrt_u2u\", olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg(\n    function=lambda x: np.sqrt(x), in_fmt=FixFormat(0,0,16), out_fmt=FixFormat(0,0,16),\n    offs_fmt=FixFormat(0,0,18), grad_fmt=FixFormat(0,4,10), points=256, name=\"sqrt_u2u\",\n    valid_range=(0.25, 1.0)))\ncheck(\"tanh_s2s\", olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg(\n    function=lambda x: np.tanh(3*x), in_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,16), out_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,16),\n    offs_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,18), grad_fmt=FixFormat(0,2,12), points=256, name=\"tanh_s2s\"))\ncheck(\"wide_s\", olo_fix_lin_approx_cfg(\n    function=lambda x: np.sin(x), in_fmt=FixFormat(1,2,10), out_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,12),\n    offs_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,14), grad_fmt=FixFormat(1,0,12), points=64, name=\"wide_s\"))\nEOF"

## Assistant <sup>9/4/2026, 11:42:52 AM</sup>

Added to [olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.md](doc/fix/olo_fix_lin_approx_calc.md):

```text
segment_width  = 2**(S + I) / TableSize_g
index          = floor(In_Data / segment_width) mod TableSize_g
segment_center = (floor(In_Data / segment_width) + 0.5) * segment_width

Out_Result     = offset[index] + gradient[index] * (In_Data - segment_center)
```

Two notes on what I included beyond the literal ask:

- `segment_width` is needed for `index` to mean anything, so it comes along.
- I also defined `segment_center`, which the `Out_Result` line already referenced without ever defining (it was written `segment_center[index]`). Defining it directly from `In_Data` rather than indexed by `index` avoids a trap: the unwrapped `floor()` is what gives the correct centre, whereas `index` has the `mod` applied. For a signed input of `-1.0` those differ — `index` is `TableSize_g/2` but the centre is negative. If you'd rather keep the change minimal, dropping the `segment_center` line is fine, but then the `Out_Result` line stays partly undefined.

The `mod TableSize_g` is what expresses the signed wrap, so it ties directly into the paragraph below about negative inputs landing in the upper half of the table.

I verified the formulas against the model on three configurations — unsigned `(0,0,16)`, signed `(1,0,16)`, and signed with integer bits `(1,2,10)`. `index` matches `_table_index()` exactly and `segment_center` matches `centers[index]` exactly in all three; the `Out_Result` line differs only by quantization (0.5-2.8 LSB), as expected since the doc formula is exact arithmetic.
